- nit: lib/pattern/no_reduce_for_map_building.ex:40 — The documentation says only anonymous-function bodies are matched, but the newly added example uses capture syntax, which the rule also supports; maintainers are given contradictory guidance about admitted inputs.
## lib/pattern/no_reduce_while_without_halt.ex — 2026-08-25 (unchanged_rule, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_reduce_while_without_halt.ex:71 — The rule treats every `Enum.reduce_while` AST as Elixir’s standard `Enum`, even when the file has `alias SomeModule, as: Enum`. On such valid input it rewrites `SomeModule.reduce_while/3` to `SomeModule.reduce/3`; those functions need not be equivalent, so the fix can change the program’s result.
## lib/pattern/no_redundant_case_nil_clause.ex — 2026-08-25 (modified, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_redundant_case_nil_clause.ex:172 — Pre-existing: an underscore-prefixed variable is treated as a wildcard even though it binds the matched value. If both duplicate bodies reference that name, the `nil` body can read an outer binding while the final clause reads its newly bound subject; `Macro.to_string/1` still considers the bodies identical. Removing the `nil` clause then changes results—for example, with outer `_fallback = :outer`, `nil -> _fallback; n when is_integer(n) -> n; _fallback -> _fallback` returns `:outer` before the fix and `nil` afterward.
## lib/pattern/no_redundant_comparison_guard.ex — 2026-08-25 (modified, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_redundant_comparison_guard.ex:69 — pre-existing: clauses are grouped only by function name and arity, not by enclosing module. Given module A with `f(n) when is_number(n) and n < 0` and module B with `f(n) when is_number(n) and n >= 0`, the rule treats A’s clause as preceding B’s and removes B’s comparison; B then incorrectly accepts negative numbers.
## lib/pattern/no_redundant_dedup_before_mapset.ex — 2026-08-25 (unchanged_rule, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_redundant_dedup_before_mapset.ex:90 — The pipe matcher accepts `MapSet.new/2`, but its replacement always emits `MapSet.new/1`. For `Enum.uniq(items) |> MapSet.new(transform)`, the fix deletes `transform`, producing a different set and skipping any side effects or exceptions from the function. The non-pipe matcher at line 102 also accepts `MapSet.new(Enum.uniq(items), transform)` and removes deduplication even though that changes how many times a stateful or raising transform runs.
## lib/pattern/no_redundant_enum_join_separator.ex — 2026-08-25 (unchanged_rule, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_redundant_enum_join_separator.ex:27 — The rule treats every `Enum` alias AST as the standard library module. On input such as `alias MyEnum, as: Enum`, where `MyEnum.join/2` and `MyEnum.join/1` intentionally return different values, it rewrites `Enum.join(list, "")` to `Enum.join(list)` and changes the program’s answer; the same defect affects `map_join`.
## lib/pattern/no_redundant_list_traversal.ex — 2026-08-25 (modified, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_redundant_list_traversal.ex:498 — pre-existing: two assignments may bind the same result variable, such as `x = Enum.min(numbers); x = Enum.max(numbers)`. The fix emits `{x, x} = Enum.min_max(numbers)`, which raises `MatchError` whenever the minimum and maximum differ instead of returning the original maximum.
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_redundant_list_traversal.ex:235 — pre-existing: an inline call uses the hard-coded binding `minimum` or `maximum` without checking whether that name is already live. For example, after `maximum = 100`, fixing `minimum = Enum.min(numbers); minimum + Enum.max(numbers) + maximum` overwrites `maximum` with the list maximum and changes the answer.
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_redundant_list_traversal.ex:309 — pre-existing: rebinding is checked only strictly between the two traversal statements, so the first traversal assignment itself may overwrite the traversed variable. `numbers = Enum.min(numbers); maximum = Enum.max(numbers)` originally calls `Enum.max/1` on the rebound scalar, but the fix evaluates `Enum.min_max/1` on the original enumerable and changes a failure into a result.
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_redundant_list_traversal.ex:276 — pre-existing: the matcher accepts any plain-variable enumerable, not only immutable lists. Collapsing two traversals of a stateful or one-shot enumerable into one changes enumeration side effects and potentially returned values, violating equivalence for admitted inputs.
- nit: lib/pattern/no_redundant_list_traversal.ex:13 — the moduledoc says `length/1`, `Enum.count/1`, and `Enum.sum/1` are still tracked, but `@tracked_enum_funcs` contains only `:min` and `:max` and the `length/1` matcher was removed.
## test/pattern/no_redundant_list_traversal_check_test.exs — 2026-08-25 (modified, test_pattern)
- nit: test/pattern/no_redundant_list_traversal_check_test.exs:3 — The moduledoc says every negative uses `Enum.min`/`Enum.max`, but the four scope negatives at lines 145–183 do not, making the test rationale self-contradictory.
- nit: test/pattern/no_redundant_list_traversal_check_test.exs:297 — `Enum.min/2` and `Enum.max/2` take an empty-enumerable fallback in Elixir 1.17, not a custom sorter; custom comparators use arity 3, so the test name and mixed-arity explanation misdescribe the input and leave the claimed custom-comparator case untested.
## test/pattern/no_redundant_list_traversal_fix_test.exs — 2026-08-25 (modified, test_pattern)
- concern: test/pattern/no_redundant_list_traversal_fix_test.exs:197 — The different-variable, rebinding, different-block, non-variable-argument, arity-2, and discarded-assignment safety tests still use only `length`/`Enum.count`/`Enum.sum`, which the rule no longer tracks. They now pass vacuously even if the corresponding fix-safety mechanisms break; each fixture must use the tracked `Enum.min`/`Enum.max` pair.
- nit: test/pattern/no_redundant_list_traversal_fix_test.exs:152 — Two newly rewritten fixtures assign `spread` or `ratio` but then reference undefined `average`, so they are not realistic valid programs and cannot support a semantic safety claim.
## lib/pattern/no_redundant_local_capture.ex — 2026-08-25 (unchanged_rule, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_redundant_local_capture.ex:97 — Nested calls such as `f = &foo/1; f.(f.(x))` produce overlapping patches for both the inner and outer applications; applying overlapping Sourceror ranges can corrupt the output or crash the fix pipeline.
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_redundant_local_capture.ex:183 — The reference scan descends into quoted code. For `f = &foo/1` followed by `quote do: f.(x)`, the rule removes the runtime capture and changes the returned AST from `f.(x)` to `foo(x)`, even though the `f` inside `quote` is not the captured variable.
## lib/pattern/no_redundant_negated_guard.ex — 2026-08-25 (modified, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_redundant_negated_guard.ex:211 — Pre-existing: the rule treats every equality/inequality operator pairing as complementary. With `x === y` followed by `x != y`, input `1, 1.0` matches neither clause before repair but matches the now-unguarded second clause afterward, changing the answer.
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_redundant_negated_guard.ex:136 — Pre-existing: matching variable names does not prove the clauses compare the same values or admit the same inputs. For `def f([x], y) when x == y` followed by `def f(x, y) when x != y`, input `{[1], [1]}` has no match before repair but reaches the second clause after its guard is removed.
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_redundant_negated_guard.ex:206 — Pre-existing: clauses are grouped only by function name and arity, without module scope, so an equality clause in one module can cause the rule to remove an inequality guard from a same-named function in another module, changing that unrelated function’s dispatch.
## lib/pattern/no_redundant_to_list.ex — 2026-08-25 (unchanged_rule, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_redundant_to_list.ex:49 — The rule also rewrites `MapSet.new(Enum.to_list(items), transform)`, but this is not equivalent for effectful enumerables: `Enum.to_list/1` exhausts the enumerable before `transform` runs, while the replacement interleaves enumeration and transformation. A stream producer and transform communicating through the process dictionary can therefore produce a different set.
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_redundant_to_list.ex:30 — The matcher treats any aliases spelled `Enum`, `Map`, or `MapSet` as the standard-library modules without resolving them. With `alias MyEnum, as: Enum` or `alias MySet, as: MapSet`, it removes a potentially meaningful custom `to_list/1` call and changes the program’s result.
## lib/pattern/no_repeated_div_rem.ex — 2026-08-25 (modified, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_repeated_div_rem.ex:66 — Declaring `unsafe_in_dsl/0` as `[]` leaves cross-boundary Ash rewrites unguarded. For `def f(x) do; q = div(x, 2); expr(div(x, 2)); end`, the matcher treats the ordinary Kernel call and the Ash-interpreted call as identical and rewrites the latter to `expr(q)`, changing a DSL division of field `x` into a reference to field `q`.
## lib/pattern/no_sort_for_top_k.ex — 2026-08-25 (modified, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_sort_for_top_k.ex:245 — pre-existing: the check admits `Enum.sort(list, &>=/2) |> Enum.at(0)` and recommends `Enum.min`, but that descending sort returns the maximum; the fixer then declines the same input because it only supports `sort/1`, leaving a false diagnostic whose suggested rewrite gives the wrong answer.
## lib/pattern/no_sort_then_at.ex — 2026-08-25 (modified, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_sort_then_at.ex:232 — The `:desc`/first rewrite changes which tied value is returned: `Enum.sort([1, 1.0], :desc) |> Enum.at(0)` returns the first stable maximum (`1`), but `Enum.max([1, 1.0], &>/2, fn -> nil end)` keeps the last (`1.0`), so the fix is not `===`-equivalent.
## test/pattern/no_sort_then_at_fix_test.exs — 2026-08-25 (modified, test_pattern)
- blocker: test/pattern/no_sort_then_at_fix_test.exs:51 — The test blesses a behavior-changing repair for descending-first input. With `nums = [1, 1.0]`, stable `Enum.sort(nums, :desc) |> Enum.at(0)` returns the first tied maximum (`1`), while `Enum.max(nums, &>/2, fn -> nil end)` selects the last (`1.0`). The same incorrect expectation appears for piped and anonymous descending-first forms.
## lib/pattern/no_sort_then_reverse.ex — 2026-08-25 (modified, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_sort_then_reverse.ex:7 — pre-existing: the claimed equivalence fails for mixed numerically equal terms. For `[1, 1.0]`, ascending stable sort followed by reverse returns `[1.0, 1]`, while `Enum.sort(list, :desc)` preserves the original tie order as `[1, 1.0]`, changing the exact result.
## lib/pattern/no_string_concat_in_loop.ex — 2026-08-25 (unchanged_rule, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_string_concat_in_loop.ex:78 — The rewrite changes behavior when an element or mapped expression is not a binary: `acc <> value` raises, while `Enum.join/1` and `Enum.map_join/2` convert values implementing `String.Chars` (for example integers), so previously failing input can return a string.
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_string_concat_in_loop.ex:74 — Matching the syntactic alias `Enum` does not prove the call targets Elixir’s `Enum`; after `alias MyEnum, as: Enum`, the rule rewrites `MyEnum.reduce/3` into `MyEnum.join/1` or `map_join/2`, which can change behavior or introduce an undefined-function failure.
## lib/pattern/no_string_length_for_char_check.ex — 2026-08-25 (unchanged_rule, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_string_length_for_char_check.ex:72 — The matcher treats any `String.length/1` syntax as Elixir’s standard `String`, but `String` can be rebound with `alias OtherModule, as: String`. If that module’s `length/1` and `graphemes/1` differ, the rewrite changes the boolean result; for example, `length/1` returning `1` and `graphemes/1` returning `[]` changes `true` to `false`.
## lib/pattern/no_take_while_length_check.ex — 2026-08-25 (modified, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_take_while_length_check.ex:173 — pre-existing: the generated callback binds `elem` and `acc`, so a predicate that references an outer variable with either name is silently changed; for example, `Enum.take_while(items, &(&1 > elem)) |> length()` compares each item with itself after repair instead of with the original outer `elem`, producing a wrong count.
## lib/pattern/no_tautological_if.ex — 2026-08-25 (modified, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_tautological_if.ex:147 — pre-existing: branch comparison removes all AST metadata, but metadata can affect meaning. For `if false, do: __ENV__.line, else: __ENV__.line`, the branches are treated as identical even though they return their distinct source lines; replacing the `if` with the first branch therefore changes the result.
## lib/pattern/no_trailing_newline_in_doc.ex — 2026-08-25 (modified, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_trailing_newline_in_doc.ex:123 — A doc containing an escaped literal backslash before `n` internally and a real trailing `\n`, such as `@doc "Path C:\\new.\n"`, is incorrectly ignored. The raw check treats every internal `\n` byte pair as a newline without checking backslash parity, so this admitted trailing-newline case is neither reported nor repaired.
## test/pattern/no_trailing_newline_in_doc_check_test.exs — 2026-08-25 (modified, test_pattern)
- concern: test/pattern/no_trailing_newline_in_doc_check_test.exs:227 — The test says the fix is byte-identical, but `confirm_fix/2` trims all trailing newline bytes before comparing. It would still pass if the fix added or removed newlines at end-of-file; use an exact `==` assertion for this claim.
## lib/pattern/no_underscore_function_name.ex — 2026-08-25 (modified, rule_pattern)
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_underscore_function_name.ex:78 — pre-existing: the rule admits public `def _helper`, then renames its exported API to `do_helper`; callers in other modules remain unchanged and fail with `undefined function _helper/...`.
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_underscore_function_name.ex:207 — pre-existing: when a local `_helper` exists, the atom-wide rewrite also changes unrelated uses such as `Other._helper()` and the data literal `:_helper`, altering external calls and returned data.
- blocker: lib/pattern/no_underscore_function_name.ex:221 — pre-existing: the rule never checks whether the suggested `do_helper` already exists; fixing a module containing both names merges their clauses under one name, changing dispatch or causing a compile error.
